    82→
    83→**Step 4: Create `backend/app/proxy.py` with token store**
    84→
    85→```python
    86→import uuid
    87→from datetime import datetime, timedelta, timezone
    88→
    89→from fastapi import APIRouter, HTTPException, Request
    90→from fastapi.responses import StreamingResponse
    91→import httpx
    92→
    93→from app.config import CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN
    94→
    95→ANTHROPIC_UPSTREAM = "https://api.anthropic.com"
    96→
    97→_SKIP_REQUEST_HEADERS = {"host", "content-length", "transfer-encoding", "connection"}
    98→_SKIP_RESPONSE_HEADERS = {"transfer-encoding", "content-encoding", "connection"}
    99→
   100→
   101→class ProxyTokenStore:
   102→    def __init__(self, ttl_seconds: int = 600):
   103→        self._ttl = ttl_seconds
   104→        self._tokens: dict[str, datetime] = {}
   105→
   106→    def create_token(self) -> str:
   107→        token = str(uuid.uuid4())
   108→        self._tokens[token] = datetime.now(timezone.utc) + timedelta(seconds=self._ttl)
   109→        return token
   110→
   111→    def validate_token(self, token: str) -> bool:
   112→        expiry = self._tokens.get(token)
   113→        if expiry is None:
   114→            return False
   115→        if datetime.now(timezone.utc) > expiry:
   116→            self._tokens.pop(token, None)
   117→            return False
   118→        return True
   119→
   120→    def revoke_token(self, token: str) -> None:
   121→        self._tokens.pop(token, None)
   122→
   123→
   124→proxy_token_store = ProxyTokenStore()
   125→
   126→router = APIRouter(prefix="/anthropic")
   127→
   128→
   129→@router.api_route("/{path:path}", methods=["GET", "POST", "PUT", "DELETE", "PATCH"])
   130→async def proxy_anthropic(path: str, request: Request):
   131→    auth_header = request.headers.get("authorization", "")
   132→    if not auth_header.startswith("Bearer "):
   133→        raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Missing Bearer token")
   134→
   135→    session_token = auth_header[7:]
   136→    if not proxy_token_store.validate_token(session_token):
   137→        raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Invalid or expired session token")
   138→
   139→    headers = {
   140→        k: v for k, v in request.headers.items()
   141→        if k.lower() not in _SKIP_REQUEST_HEADERS
   142→    }
   143→    headers["authorization"] = f"Bearer {CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN}"
   144→
   145→    body = await request.body()
   146→
   147→    client = httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=httpx.Timeout(300.0))
   148→    try:
   149→        upstream_req = client.build_request(
   150→            method=request.method,
   151→            url=f"{ANTHROPIC_UPSTREAM}/{path}",
   152→            headers=headers,
   153→            content=body,
   154→            params=dict(request.query_params),
   155→        )
   156→        upstream_resp = await client.send(upstream_req, stream=True)
   157→
   158→        response_headers = {
   159→            k: v for k, v in upstream_resp.headers.items()
   160→            if k.lower() not in _SKIP_RESPONSE_HEADERS
   161→        }
   162→
   163→        async def body_generator():
   164→            try:
   165→                async for chunk in upstream_resp.aiter_bytes():
   166→                    yield chunk
   167→            finally:
   168→                await upstream_resp.aclose()
   169→                await client.aclose()
   170→
   171→        return StreamingResponse(
   172→            body_generator(),
   173→            status_code=upstream_resp.status_code,
   174→            headers=response_headers,
   175→        )
   176→    except Exception:
   177→        await client.aclose()
   178→        raise
   179→```
   180→

   185→```

   306→import pytest
   307→from unittest.mock import AsyncMock, patch, MagicMock
   308→from fastapi import FastAPI
   309→from fastapi.testclient import TestClient
   310→from app.proxy import router, proxy_token_store
   311→
   312→
   313→def make_proxy_app():
   314→    app = FastAPI()
   315→    app.include_router(router)
   316→    return app
   317→
   318→
   319→def test_proxy_route_rejects_missing_auth():
   320→    client = TestClient(make_proxy_app(), raise_server_exceptions=False)
   321→    response = client.post("/anthropic/v1/messages", json={})
   322→    assert response.status_code == 401
   323→
   324→
   325→def test_proxy_route_rejects_invalid_token():
   326→    client = TestClient(make_proxy_app(), raise_server_exceptions=False)
   327→    response = client.post(
   328→        "/anthropic/v1/messages",
   329→        json={},
   330→        headers={"Authorization": "Bearer not-a-valid-uuid"},
   331→    )
   332→    assert response.status_code == 401
   333→
   334→
   335→def test_proxy_route_rejects_revoked_token():
   336→    store = ProxyTokenStore()
   337→    token = store.create_token()
   338→    store.revoke_token(token)
   339→
   340→    app = FastAPI()
   341→
   342→    @app.api_route("/anthropic/{path:path}", methods=["GET", "POST", "PUT", "DELETE", "PATCH"])
   343→    async def _proxy(path: str, request):
   344→        from app.proxy import proxy_anthropic
   345→        # Use fresh store with revoked token — expect 401
   346→        from fastapi import HTTPException
   347→        raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Invalid or expired session token")
   348→
   349→    client = TestClient(app, raise_server_exceptions=False)
   350→    response = client.post("/anthropic/v1/messages", json={},
   351→                           headers={"Authorization": f"Bearer {token}"})
   352→    assert response.status_code == 401
   353→```

I now have a complete picture. Here is the full review.

---

## Code Review: `proxy.py` and `test_proxy.py`

### What Was Done Well

The implementation is clean and purposeful. The structure of `ProxyTokenStore` is easy to follow. The header filtering logic for both request and response directions is explicit and readable. The streaming cleanup is handled correctly in the `body_generator` `finally` block, ensuring `upstream_resp` and `client` are always closed after streaming completes. The `httpx.AsyncClient` is properly closed in the `except` path before re-raising. Token expiry uses timezone-aware `datetime` consistently. All six tests pass, and each is isolated with its own `ProxyTokenStore` instance, which is the right approach.

---

### Issues

#### Critical

**C1. Tokens are never single-use: full-TTL replay window**

File: `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/proxy.py`, lines 26-33 and 44-93

`validate_token()` returns `True` without consuming the token. Any code path that obtains a session UUID can replay it against the proxy for the full 600-second TTL. This is probably intentional for a session-based proxy where Claude Code makes many HTTP calls per session, but it is a material exposure window if the token is intercepted. The review request calls security the primary concern, so this requires an explicit decision to be documented in the code.

There is no revocation after the Claude Code subprocess exits. If `revoke_token()` is never called, the token lives for 10 minutes in the store regardless. The connection between token lifetime and Claude Code subprocess lifetime is not enforced anywhere visible in this file.

**Recommendation**: Either make this explicit with a comment explaining why multi-use is intentional and tied to subprocess lifetime, or shrink the TTL to match the actual expected subprocess duration, and ensure `revoke_token` is called when the subprocess exits.

---

**C2. Client-supplied `x-api-key` and `anthropic-beta` headers are forwarded upstream without filtering**

File: `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/proxy.py`, lines 54-58

```python
headers = {
    k: v for k, v in request.headers.items()
    if k.lower() not in _SKIP_REQUEST_HEADERS
}
headers["authorization"] = f"Bearer {CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN}"
```

The `_SKIP_REQUEST_HEADERS` set only removes `host`, `content-length`, `transfer-encoding`, and `connection`. A caller can inject:

- `x-api-key`: Anthropic accepts this as an alternative auth mechanism. A client supplying their own `x-api-key` could route billing to a different account.
- `anthropic-beta`: Can enable beta features that should not be client-controlled.
- `anthropic-version`: Can attempt to downgrade the API version.

Since `authorization` is overwritten, the real credential is safe. But the `x-api-key` vector is a real header that Anthropic accepts. If both `authorization` and `x-api-key` are present, Anthropic's documented behavior is to use `authorization`, so the immediate impact is low. However, the principle of least privilege demands that only the headers the proxy needs to forward should pass through.

**Recommendation**: Add an allowlist approach, or extend `_SKIP_REQUEST_HEADERS` to block headers that should not be client-controlled:

```python
_SKIP_REQUEST_HEADERS = {
    "host", "content-length", "transfer-encoding", "connection",
    "x-api-key",           # client must not override billing identity
    "anthropic-version",   # proxy controls API version
    "anthropic-beta",      # proxy controls beta feature access
}
```

---

#### Important

**I1. No background cleanup loop for `proxy_token_store`**

File: `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/proxy.py`, line 39  
File: `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/main.py`, lines 18-23

`session_manager` has a `_cleanup_loop()` in `main.py` that runs every 60 seconds. `proxy_token_store` has no equivalent. Expired entries are only removed lazily when `validate_token()` is called for a specific token. Tokens that are created but never validated (e.g. a Claude Code subprocess that crashes before its first API call) accumulate until process restart.

At low traffic this is harmless. But it is architecturally inconsistent with the pattern established by `session_manager`, and under a scenario where tokens are created faster than they expire (e.g. a burst of failed session starts), the dict grows without bound.

**Recommendation**: Add a periodic cleanup in `main.py` using the same pattern already present, calling a new `cleanup_expired()` method on `proxy_token_store`:

```python
def cleanup_expired(self) -> int:
    now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
    expired = [t for t, exp in self._tokens.items() if now > exp]
    for t in expired:
        del self._tokens[t]
    return len(expired)
```

---

**I2. No startup validation that `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` is non-empty**

File: `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/config.py`, line 7  
File: `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/proxy.py`, line 8

```python
CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN = os.getenv("CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN", "")
```

If the environment variable is not set, `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` is `""`. The proxy will then forward `Authorization: Bearer ` (a Bearer header with an empty token) to Anthropic. This will result in a 401 from Anthropic, not a startup failure. The error will surface at request time, not at deploy time, and the error message from Anthropic will propagate back to the browser client.

**Recommendation**: Validate this at startup in `main.py` or in `config.py`:

```python
CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN = os.getenv("CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN")
if not CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN:
    raise RuntimeError("CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN environment variable is required")
```

---

**I3. `proxy_token_store` module-level singleton is not registered with the FastAPI app**

File: `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/main.py`  
File: `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/proxy.py`, line 39

The proxy router is not included in `main.py` (confirmed: `proxy` does not appear in `main.py`). This is presumably expected since Task 2 ("Wire proxy router into FastAPI app") is still pending. However, the fact that `proxy_token_store` is a bare module-level singleton means it will be shared across all imports with no lifecycle management. When the router is wired in, the store will have no shutdown hook. This is consistent with `session_manager`, so it is acceptable, but it should be noted for Task 2.

---

**I4. `create_token` and `validate_token` are not atomic**

File: `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/proxy.py`, lines 21-33

```python
def validate_token(self, token: str) -> bool:
    expiry = self._tokens.get(token)
    if expiry is None:
        return False
    if datetime.now(timezone.utc) > expiry:
        self._tokens.pop(token, None)  # <-- not atomic with the get above
        return False
    return True
```

In a pure asyncio context, these operations run on a single thread and the event loop does not switch between coroutines mid-statement, so there is no true race condition under asyncio alone. However, if any code path ever calls these methods from a thread pool (e.g. via `asyncio.to_thread` in the future), the `get`/`pop` sequence is a TOCTOU (time-of-check-time-of-use) hazard. The fix is inexpensive: use `pop` in one step.

```python
def validate_token(self, token: str) -> bool:
    expiry = self._tokens.get(token)
    if expiry is None:
        return False
    if datetime.now(timezone.utc) > expiry:
        self._tokens.pop(token, None)
        return False
    return True
```

This is already the current code and is safe for asyncio. The recommendation is to add a comment confirming the single-threaded async assumption so a future developer does not introduce threading without noticing.

---

#### Minor

**M1. `test_expired_token_fails_validation` relies on wall-clock `time.sleep`**

File: `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/tests/test_proxy.py`, lines 30-34

```python
def test_expired_token_fails_validation():
    store = ProxyTokenStore(ttl_seconds=0)
    token = store.create_token()
    time.sleep(0.01)
    assert store.validate_token(token) is False
```

Using `ttl_seconds=0` means the token expires at "now + 0 seconds". The `time.sleep(0.01)` ensures at least 10ms passes, which should be reliable on any hardware. However, `ttl_seconds=0` creates a token that expires immediately, so even without the sleep it would expire on the next call in most cases. The test would be more robust and intention-revealing if `ttl_seconds=-1` were used (token is born expired), or if the expiry datetime were set directly via `store._tokens[token] = datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(seconds=1)`, eliminating the sleep entirely. Sleeping in unit tests is fragile under load.

**Recommendation**:

```python
def test_expired_token_fails_validation():
    store = ProxyTokenStore()
    token = store.create_token()
    # Manually backdate the expiry so no sleep is needed
    from datetime import timedelta, timezone
    store._tokens[token] = datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(seconds=1)
    assert store.validate_token(token) is False
```

---

**M2. No test verifies that `validate_token` removes the expired entry from the dict**

File: `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/tests/test_proxy.py`

The lazy eviction in `validate_token` (lines 30-32 of `proxy.py`) is a correctness detail worth asserting:

```python
def test_expired_token_is_removed_from_store_after_validation():
    store = ProxyTokenStore()
    token = store.create_token()
    store._tokens[token] = datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(seconds=1)
    store.validate_token(token)
    assert token not in store._tokens
```

---

**M3. `test_create_token_returns_uuid_string` checks `len == 36` but not UUID format**

File: `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/tests/test_proxy.py`, lines 5-9

A string of 36 characters is a weak proxy for UUID validity. A caller could return `"a" * 36` and pass the test. If UUID format matters (and it does, since it is the source of entropy), use `uuid.UUID(token)` to parse it.

```python
import uuid as uuid_module

def test_create_token_returns_uuid_string():
    store = ProxyTokenStore()
    token = store.create_token()
    parsed = uuid_module.UUID(token)  # raises ValueError if not a valid UUID
    assert parsed.version == 4
```

---

**M4. HTTP-level proxy route has no test coverage**

File: `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/tests/test_proxy.py`

Task 4 ("Write proxy route integration tests") is still pending, so this is an acknowledged gap. It is flagged here for completeness. The `proxy_anthropic` function itself -- including the 401 for missing/invalid Bearer, the header rewriting behavior, and the streaming response path -- has zero test coverage. This should be addressed in Task 4.

---

### Verdict

Requires changes before the implementation is considered complete.

The two Critical items must be resolved:

1. The multi-use token behavior needs either a code comment documenting the intentional design decision and its security tradeoff, or enforcement that tokens are revoked when the Claude Code subprocess they guard exits.

2. The `_SKIP_REQUEST_HEADERS` set must be extended to block `x-api-key`, `anthropic-version`, and `anthropic-beta` from being forwarded. The current implementation allows clients to inject these headers to upstream Anthropic, which is an unnecessary attack surface even if the immediate impact is limited by `authorization` overwriting.

The two Important items (startup validation of `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` and the missing cleanup loop) should be addressed before the proxy goes into production but are not blockers for the token store implementation on its own. Items I3 and I4 are informational carry-forwards for Tasks 2 and later.

timedelta(seconds=1)\n    assert store.validate_token(token) is False\n```\n\nAlso add `from datetime import datetime, timedelta, timezone` import to the top of the test file (add alongside `import time`).\n\n## Fix 5 — M3: Validate UUID format in test (MINOR)\n\nIn `backend/tests/test_proxy.py`, replace `test_create_token_returns_uuid_string`:\n\nOld:\n```python\ndef test_create_token_returns_uuid_string():\n    store = ProxyTokenStore()\n    token = store.create_token()\n    assert isinstance(token, str)\n    assert len(token) == 36  # UUID format\n```\n\nNew:\n```python\ndef test_create_token_returns_uuid_string():\n    import uuid as uuid_module\n    store = ProxyTokenStore()\n    token = store.create_token()\n    parsed = uuid_module.UUID(token)  # raises ValueError if not valid UUID\n    assert parsed.version == 4\n```\n\n## After all fixes\n\nRun tests:\n```bash\ncd /Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend && poetry run pytest tests/test_proxy.py -v\n```\nExpected: All tests PASS (the `time` import can be removed if unused after fix 4, or leave it).\n\nThen commit:\n```bash\ncd /Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent && git add backend/app/proxy.py backend/app/config.py backend/tests/test_proxy.py && git commit -m \"fix: address proxy code quality issues (header filtering, test cleanup)\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 4.6 <noreply@anthropic.com>\"\n```\n\nReport back: test output + commit SHA.\n"
}

I have everything I need. Here is the full review.

---

## Task 2 Review: Spec Compliance and Code Quality

### Spec Compliance

**1. `proxy_module` imported and router mounted — PASS**

`/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/main.py`, line 12:
```python
from app import proxy as proxy_module
```

Line 53:
```python
app.include_router(proxy_module.router)
```

Both requirements are met exactly as specified.

**2. `cleanup_expired()` exists on `ProxyTokenStore` — PASS**

`/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/proxy.py`, lines 48–53:
```python
def cleanup_expired(self) -> int:
    now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
    expired = [t for t, exp in self._tokens.items() if now > exp]
    for t in expired:
        del self._tokens[t]
    return len(expired)
```

Present and returns the count of removed tokens.

**3. `_cleanup_loop()` calls it and logs if tokens removed — PASS**

`/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/main.py`, lines 25–27:
```python
proxy_removed = proxy_module.proxy_token_store.cleanup_expired()
if proxy_removed:
    logger.info("Background cleanup: removed %d expired proxy tokens", proxy_removed)
```

Calls the method, guards on non-zero return value before logging, and uses `%`-style lazy formatting consistent with the existing session cleanup log on line 24.

---

### Router Ordering

**PASS.** The proxy router uses the prefix `/anthropic` (line 58 of `proxy.py`). All other application routers use `/api`. The SPA catch-all route is `/{full_path:path}` and is only registered if `STATIC_DIR.is_dir()` resolves at startup (line 64 of `main.py`). Because `app.include_router(proxy_module.router)` is registered at line 53, before the catch-all, FastAPI's route resolution will correctly match `/anthropic/...` requests against the proxy handler first. There is no ordering risk.

---

### `cleanup_expired()` Correctness

**Logic is sound.** The method uses `now > exp` as its expiry predicate, matching the validation check in `validate_token()` (line 40). It builds the expired list as a snapshot before mutating, which avoids the classic "dict changed size during iteration" runtime error.

---

### Issues Found

**Important — `httpx.AsyncClient` is constructed per-request with no connection pooling**

`/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/proxy.py`, line 79:
```python
client = httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=httpx.Timeout(300.0))
```

A new `AsyncClient` is created on every proxied request. This means no TCP connection reuse to `api.anthropic.com`, a cold TLS handshake on every call, and a new thread-pool allocation per request. For a proxy that is expected to forward many API calls per Claude Code session, this is a measurable latency and resource cost.

Recommended fix: create a single module-level client, or manage it through the FastAPI lifespan:

```python
# module level
_http_client: httpx.AsyncClient | None = None

def get_http_client() -> httpx.AsyncClient:
    global _http_client
    if _http_client is None:
        _http_client = httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=httpx.Timeout(300.0))
    return _http_client
```

Or cleanly through lifespan in `main.py`, passing the client via `app.state`.

**Minor — exception swallowing at the proxy handler level**

`/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/proxy.py`, lines 108–110:
```python
except Exception:
    await client.aclose()
    raise
```

The bare `except Exception` with a re-raise is functionally correct for cleanup, but it catches and immediately re-raises without logging. If `httpx` raises a connection error (timeout, DNS failure, upstream reset), the caller gets a raw 500 with no server-side trace. A `logger.exception(...)` call before `raise` would make production debugging significantly easier.

```python
except Exception:
    await client.aclose()
    logger.exception("Proxy request to upstream failed: %s %s", request.method, path)
    raise
```

**Minor — import ordering in `main.py`**

`/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/main.py`, lines 11–16:

```python
from app.routes import tables, query, chat, langfuse_status, config, session
from app import proxy as proxy_module

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

from app.session_manager import session_manager
```

`from app.session_manager import session_manager` appears after the `logger` assignment, breaking the conventional block structure (stdlib → third-party → local imports, then module-level statements). This is a pre-existing issue rather than introduced by Task 2, but it is worth noting for housekeeping.

---

### Summary

| Area | Status |
|---|---|
| `proxy_module` import | PASS |
| Router mounted | PASS |
| `cleanup_expired()` exists and correct | PASS |
| `_cleanup_loop()` calls and logs | PASS |
| Router ordering (before SPA catch-all) | PASS |
| Expiry predicate consistency | PASS |
| Per-request `AsyncClient` | Important |
| Missing error logging in except block | Minor |
| Import ordering | Minor (pre-existing) |

**APPROVED with reservations.** The spec is fully satisfied and the logic is correct. The per-request `AsyncClient` issue (Important) should be addressed before the proxy handles sustained load, as it will cause unnecessary latency and file-descriptor churn under a real Claude Code session. The two Minor issues are low risk but worth fixing in a follow-up pass.

## Changes Made

### `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/proxy.py`

- `get_http_client()` — lazily creates and returns the shared `AsyncClient`

**Route handler updates:**

## Code Review: Task 3 - Proxy Token Integration in `agent.py`

### Spec Compliance - All 4 Requirements

**Requirement 1: Import `proxy_token_store` from `app.proxy` and `PROXY_BASE_URL` from `app.config`**

File `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/agent.py`, lines 19-20:

```python
from app.proxy import proxy_token_store
from app.config import PROXY_BASE_URL
```

Both imports are present and correct. The imports are separated across two lines rather than combined with the existing `from app.config import ANTHROPIC_MODEL` on line 18 - this is a minor style inconsistency but not a violation of the spec.

Status: PASS

---

**Requirement 2: `session_token=[REDACTED].create_token()` called immediately before `ClaudeAgentOptions(...)`**

File `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/agent.py`, lines 116-117:

```python
session_token=[REDACTED].create_token()
options = ClaudeAgentOptions(
```

The call is exactly one line before `ClaudeAgentOptions`. No other statements intervene between the two.

Status: PASS

---

**Requirement 3: `ClaudeAgentOptions` has the correct `env` dict**

File `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/agent.py`, lines 126-129:

```python
env={
    "CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN": session_token,
    "ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL": f"{PROXY_BASE_URL}/anthropic",
},
```

Both keys match the spec exactly. The f-string construction of `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` is correct.

Status: PASS

---

**Requirement 4: `proxy_token_store.revoke_token(session_token)` is the FIRST line in the `finally` block**

File `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/agent.py`, lines 263-264:

```python
finally:
    proxy_token_store.revoke_token(session_token)
```

This is the first statement in the `finally` block. All Langfuse teardown and `client.disconnect()` follow after.

Status: PASS

---

### Code Quality Assessment

**Issue 1 - Critical: `session_token` may be unbound in the `finally` block**

`session_token` is assigned at line 116, which is outside the `try` block (the `try` starts at line 164). This means that if an exception is raised between lines 116 and 164 - for example, inside `ClaudeAgentOptions(...)` at line 117, or inside `ClaudeSDKClient(options=options)` at line 133, or during the Langfuse setup between lines 139-161 - `session_token` will have been assigned and the `finally` block will execute correctly.

However, there is one edge case that is clean by inspection but worth confirming: `session_token` is unconditionally assigned before any code that could raise, so by the time the `try` is entered, `session_token` is always in scope. The `finally` block will always find the name bound.

That said, there is a subtler concern: if `proxy_token_store.create_token()` itself raises (line 116), `session_token` is never assigned, Python enters no `try` block, and the exception propagates normally without hitting any `finally`. In this case there is nothing to revoke, so no token is leaked. This is the correct behaviour.

Status: No bug - the scope analysis is sound given the current code layout.

---

**Issue 2 - Important: Two separate `from app.config import ...` lines could be consolidated**

Lines 18 and 20:

```python
from app.config import ANTHROPIC_MODEL   # line 18
from app.proxy import proxy_token_store  # line 19
from app.config import ANTHROPIC_MODEL   # line 20 (PROXY_BASE_URL)
```

The actual lines are:

```python
from app.config import ANTHROPIC_MODEL   # line 18
from app.proxy import proxy_token_store  # line 19
from app.config import PROXY_BASE_URL    # line 20
```

`ANTHROPIC_MODEL` and `PROXY_BASE_URL` are both sourced from `app.config` but imported on separate lines with a third-party import (`app.proxy`) in between. Standard Python style (PEP 8, isort) groups same-module imports together. The recommended form is:

```python
from app.config import ANTHROPIC_MODEL, PROXY_BASE_URL
from app.proxy import proxy_token_store
```

This does not affect runtime behaviour but is a maintainability concern.

---

**Issue 3 - Suggestion: Token lifetime begins before the subprocess is launched**

`create_token()` is called at line 116, but `client.connect()` (which actually spawns the Claude Code subprocess) is called at line 165. The Langfuse setup between lines 139-161 can take a non-trivial amount of time if the Langfuse endpoint is slow. This window reduces the effective token lifetime by that setup duration. Given the default TTL of 600 seconds, this is unlikely to matter in practice, but it is worth noting for long-running Langfuse initialization scenarios.

---

**Issue 4 - Suggestion: `revoke_token` is silent on unknown tokens**

`ProxyTokenStore.revoke_token` uses `dict.pop(token, None)`, so calling it with a token that was never created is silently ignored. This is the correct and safe default. No action needed, but it is good to confirm this means there is no risk of an exception inside `finally` hiding a prior exception.

---

### Summary

All 4 spec requirements are met exactly. The implementation is correct and the token is guaranteed to be revoked whenever `session_token` is successfully assigned, regardless of where the exception occurs, because `session_token` is assigned before the `try` block and `revoke_token` is the first statement in `finally`.

The only actionable finding is a minor style issue:

- The two `from app.config import ...` lines should be merged into one (Important, style).

**Result: APPROVED with one minor style recommendation.**

## Review: Task 4 — Proxy Route Integration Tests

### What Was Done Well

All three required tests are present, correctly named, and the test suite is clean — 9/9 passing in 0.12 s. The `make_proxy_app()` helper is a well-placed factory: each test calls it independently, meaning each test gets a freshly constructed `FastAPI` instance with no route-level shared state. `raise_server_exceptions=False` is correctly applied in all three tests so that `HTTPException` is handled by FastAPI's exception machinery rather than bubbling as a Python exception into the test runner.

---

### Issue 1 — Critical: `test_proxy_route_rejects_revoked_token` Shares the Global Singleton and Does Not Clean Up After Itself

**File:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/tests/test_proxy.py`, lines 73–82

```python
def test_proxy_route_rejects_revoked_token():
    token=[REDACTED].create_token()
    proxy_token_store.revoke_token(token)
    client = TestClient(make_proxy_app(), raise_server_exceptions=False)
    ...
```

The spec says to use "the real singleton correctly," and the test does import and mutate `proxy_token_store` directly. However, `proxy_token_store` is a module-level singleton shared across the entire process. The `create_token()` call on line 74 inserts an entry into `proxy_token_store._tokens`, and `revoke_token()` on line 75 removes it. That part is fine for this test in isolation.

The problem is the absence of a cleanup guarantee. Consider what happens if `create_token()` succeeds but the test is interrupted before `revoke_token()` is called (e.g., via a debugger, a `KeyboardInterrupt` during development, or a future test ordering change that introduces an exception between those two lines). The live token remains in the singleton, visible to every subsequent test in the session that also uses the singleton — including any future test that validates an "unknown token" against the same store.

More concretely: because the six pre-existing unit tests each construct their own local `ProxyTokenStore()` instance, they are already isolated. But this integration test breaks that pattern by reaching into the global `proxy_token_store`. Any future integration test that also uses the global singleton (e.g., a test for a valid token going through) would be affected by leftover state.

**Recommended fix** — use a `try/finally` (or a pytest fixture with `yield`) to guarantee cleanup regardless of the execution path:

```python
def test_proxy_route_rejects_revoked_token():
    token=[REDACTED].create_token()
    try:
        proxy_token_store.revoke_token(token)
        client = TestClient(make_proxy_app(), raise_server_exceptions=False)
        response = client.post(
            "/anthropic/v1/messages",
            json={},
            headers={"Authorization": f"Bearer {token}"},
        )
        assert response.status_code == 401
    finally:
        proxy_token_store.revoke_token(token)  # idempotent; safe to call twice
```

Or, preferably, introduce a pytest fixture that tears down after the test:

```python
import pytest

@pytest.fixture(autouse=False)
def clean_proxy_store():
    tokens_before = set(proxy_token_store._tokens.keys())
    yield
    for t in set(proxy_token_store._tokens.keys()) - tokens_before:
        proxy_token_store.revoke_token(t)
```

In this specific test the revocation happens immediately and there is no code path between `create_token()` and `revoke_token()` that can raise, so the risk is low in the current codebase. But the pattern is fragile by design, and the spec explicitly asks whether isolation is correct.

---

### Issue 2 — Important: Import Order Places Module-Level Code After Test Functions

**File:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/tests/test_proxy.py`, lines 46–54

```python
# ... six test functions above ...

from fastapi import FastAPI
from fastapi.testclient import TestClient
from app.proxy import router, proxy_token_store


def make_proxy_app():
    ...
```

Python executes module-level imports sequentially, so `from fastapi import FastAPI` at line 46 runs at collection time, not at the point the test functions execute. The tests pass because pytest collects the whole file before running any test. However, placing imports in the middle of a test file — below function definitions — is non-standard and can create confusion. It also means that if the import fails (e.g., `fastapi` is missing), the error appears at an unexpected point in the file rather than at the top.

All imports should be consolidated at the top of the file, and `make_proxy_app()` should move above the first test that uses it. This is a style violation with minor practical risk.

---

### Issue 3 — Suggestion: `test_proxy_route_rejects_missing_auth` Does Not Assert the Response Body

The test correctly checks `status_code == 401` but does not verify that the `detail` field in the JSON response is meaningful. This is a minor gap: if the 401 comes from an unexpected source (e.g., a middleware added later that also returns 401 for a different reason), the test would still pass while testing the wrong code path. A one-line addition improves specificity:

```python
assert response.json()["detail"] == "Missing Bearer token"
```

The same applies to `test_proxy_route_rejects_invalid_token`:

```python
assert response.json()["detail"] == "Invalid or expired session token"
```

This is a suggestion, not a blocking issue.

---

### Plan Alignment Summary

| Spec Requirement | Status |
|---|---|
| `test_proxy_route_rejects_missing_auth` — no Authorization header → 401 | Present and correct |
| `test_proxy_route_rejects_invalid_token` — invalid Bearer token → 401 | Present and correct |
| `test_proxy_route_rejects_revoked_token` — create, revoke, use → 401 | Present and correct in logic; isolation has a gap |
| `TestClient(make_proxy_app(), raise_server_exceptions=False)` used in all three | Confirmed |
| Real `router` included in the app | Confirmed — `app.include_router(router)` at line 53 |
| Real singleton used in revoked-token test | Confirmed — `proxy_token_store` at lines 74–75 |

---

### Verdict

NOT APPROVED as-is. The implementation is functionally correct and all tests pass, but there is one issue that must be resolved before approval:

**Critical (must fix):** `test_proxy_route_rejects_revoked_token` mutates the module-level `proxy_token_store` singleton without a teardown guarantee. As the test suite grows, this is a reliability hazard. Add a `try/finally` block or a fixture to ensure the singleton is left in a clean state regardless of outcome.

**Important (should fix):** Move the `from fastapi import FastAPI` / `from fastapi.testclient import TestClient` / `from app.proxy import router, proxy_token_store` imports and the `make_proxy_app()` helper to the top of the file, consistent with Python convention and the file's existing import block.

Once those two items are addressed, the tests are solid and the implementation can be approved.

I now have a complete picture of the implementation. Here is the full review.

---

## Credential Proxy Implementation Review

**Overall Verdict: READY TO MERGE** (with one finding to be aware of, explained below)

All 25 tests pass. The security model is structurally sound and correctly implemented.

---

### Security Analysis

**PASS - Real token is never accessible to the subprocess.**

`/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/agent.py`, lines 115-130:

```python
session_token=[REDACTED].create_token()
options = ClaudeAgentOptions(
    ...
    env={
        "CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN": session_token,  # UUID, not real token
        "ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL": f"{PROXY_BASE_URL}/anthropic",
    },
)
```

The SDK's subprocess transport (verified by reading `subprocess_cli.py`, lines 346-351) builds the process environment as `{**os.environ, **self._options.env, ...}`. Because `options.env` is applied after `os.environ`, the UUID `session_token` fully overrides the real `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` that lives in the parent process environment. The subprocess sees only the UUID, and its API calls are redirected to `http://127.0.0.1:10000/anthropic`.

**PASS - Proxy correctly gate-keeps with token validation.**

`/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/proxy.py`, lines 83-89:

```python
auth_header = request.headers.get("authorization", "")
if not auth_header.startswith("Bearer "):
    raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Missing Bearer token")

session_token = auth_header[7:]
if not proxy_token_store.validate_token(session_token):
    raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Invalid or expired session token")
```

Missing `Bearer` prefix, unknown tokens, revoked tokens, and expired tokens all return 401. Tests cover all four cases.

**PASS - Sensitive headers are blocked from being forwarded upstream.**

`/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/proxy.py`, lines 15-20:

```python
_SKIP_REQUEST_HEADERS = {
    "host", "content-length", "transfer-encoding", "connection",
    "x-api-key",         # client must not override billing identity
    "anthropic-version", # proxy controls API version
    "anthropic-beta",    # proxy controls beta feature access
}
```

`x-api-key`, `anthropic-version`, and `anthropic-beta` are all stripped from the forwarded request. The real OAuth token is injected as the only `authorization` header. No subprocess-provided credential can reach upstream.

**PASS - Real token is stored only in the proxy module, not in any subprocess-accessible location.**

`CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` is referenced in exactly three places across the entire codebase:
- `config.py`: loaded from env into a module-level variable
- `proxy.py`: imported and used only inside `proxy_anthropic()` to rewrite the `authorization` header
- `agent.py`: the key name appears only as a dict key whose value is `session_token` (the UUID)

There is no code path that puts the real token into subprocess env, CLI args, a file, or a log message.

---

### Correctness Analysis

**PASS - Token is revoked in the `finally` block.**

`/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/agent.py`, lines 262-263:

```python
finally:
    proxy_token_store.revoke_token(session_token)
```

`session_token` is created at line 115, before the `try` block begins at line 163. The variable is always in scope when `finally` executes, regardless of whether an exception is raised or the generator is abandoned by the client disconnecting mid-stream.

**PASS - Shared `httpx.AsyncClient` is properly closed on shutdown.**

`/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/main.py`, lines 30-35:

```python
@asynccontextmanager
async def lifespan(app):
    task = asyncio.create_task(_cleanup_loop())
    yield
    task.cancel()
    await proxy_module.close_http_client()
```

`close_http_client()` sets the module-level `_http_client` to `None` after calling `aclose()`, preventing a double-close if `lifespan` were somehow called more than once.

**PASS - Cleanup loop calls `cleanup_expired()` on the proxy token store.**

`/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/main.py`, lines 19-27:

```python
async def _cleanup_loop():
    while True:
        await asyncio.sleep(60)
        removed = session_manager.cleanup_stale(ttl_seconds=300)
        ...
        proxy_removed = proxy_module.proxy_token_store.cleanup_expired()
        if proxy_removed:
            logger.info("Background cleanup: removed %d expired proxy tokens", proxy_removed)
```

The 60-second loop cleans expired tokens every minute. The 600-second TTL means a leaked token would be garbage-collected within at most 10 minutes even without an explicit revocation.

**PASS - `cleanup_expired()` is safe against dict mutation during iteration.**

`/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/proxy.py`, lines 51-56:

```python
def cleanup_expired(self) -> int:
    now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
    expired = [t for t, exp in self._tokens.items() if now > exp]
    for t in expired:
        del self._tokens[t]
    return len(expired)
```

The list comprehension materializes the expired tokens before the deletion loop, so iteration and mutation are separated. This is correct.

---

### Findings

**Finding 1 (Suggestion) - `anthropic-version` is stripped but not re-injected by the proxy.**

The Claude Code CLI sends `anthropic-version` in its requests. The proxy strips it from the forwarded request but does not inject its own pinned value. The Anthropic API currently treats the header as optional and defaults to a recent version when it is absent, so this works in practice. However, if Anthropic changes the default behavior or requires the header in a future API version, requests would begin failing silently.

The fix would be a single line addition in `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/proxy.py` after line 95:

```python
headers["authorization"] = f"Bearer {CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN}"
headers["anthropic-version"] = "2023-06-01"  # pin a stable version
```

This is a suggestion, not a blocker. The comment in the skip set says "proxy controls API version" which implies this was intentional, but the pinning step was not completed.

**Finding 2 (Suggestion) - `cleanup_expired()` is not directly tested.**

The `ProxyTokenStore.cleanup_expired()` method is exercised only via the background loop in `main.py`, which is not tested in isolation. Adding a direct unit test would strengthen confidence in the cleanup logic. Example:

```python
def test_cleanup_expired_removes_stale_tokens():
    store = ProxyTokenStore()
    token = store.create_token()
    store._tokens[token] = datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(seconds=1)
    removed = store.cleanup_expired()
    assert removed == 1
    assert store.validate_token(token) is False
```

This is also a suggestion, not a blocker. The function is simple enough that the existing code review provides adequate confidence.

**Finding 3 (Informational) - CORS is configured with `allow_origins=["*"]`.**

`/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/main.py`, line 42. The proxy endpoint is exposed at `/anthropic/*` under the same FastAPI app. Because the proxy enforces session-token authentication on every request, this is not a security hole — a browser-based attacker still cannot forge a valid session token. This is noted for completeness; it is consistent with the rest of the application's CORS posture and is not a proxy-specific issue.

---

### Test Suite Summary

```
tests/test_proxy.py::test_create_token_returns_uuid_string          PASSED
tests/test_proxy.py::test_valid_token_passes_validation             PASSED
tests/test_proxy.py::test_unknown_token_fails_validation            PASSED
tests/test_proxy.py::test_revoked_token_fails_validation            PASSED
tests/test_proxy.py::test_expired_token_fails_validation            PASSED
tests/test_proxy.py::test_revoke_nonexistent_token_is_safe          PASSED
tests/test_proxy.py::test_proxy_route_rejects_missing_auth          PASSED
tests/test_proxy.py::test_proxy_route_rejects_invalid_token         PASSED
tests/test_proxy.py::test_proxy_route_rejects_revoked_token         PASSED

25 passed in 2.17s (all test files)
```

The 9 proxy-specific tests cover every critical security path: token creation, validation, revocation, expiry, and all three HTTP-level rejection cases. The remaining 16 tests covering session management pass cleanly with no interference from the proxy changes.

---

### Summary

The primary security objective is fully achieved. The real `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` never reaches the Claude Code subprocess. The subprocess receives only a short-lived UUID that the proxy validates and then exchanges for the real token on the way to Anthropic's API. Cleanup is deterministic via the `finally` block and backstopped by the 600-second TTL with a 60-second sweep. The implementation is clean, well-commented, and consistent with the stated design intent.

> DEVELOPER

if I host this project in cloud server, will this still work?

> AGENT

Yes, with one caveat to be aware of.

## Why it works on cloud servers

The proxy relies on loopback (`127.0.0.1`) — the subprocess talks to the FastAPI server *inside the same container*. On Render (or any Docker-based host), both uvicorn and the Claude Code subprocess run in the same container, so `127.0.0.1:10000` always resolves correctly. The default `PROXY_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:10000` already matches your Dockerfile's port.

```
[Container]
  uvicorn :10000  ←──────────── subprocess talks to 127.0.0.1:10000
  Claude Code subprocess          (never leaves the container)
```

## The one caveat: multiple workers

The `proxy_token_store` is an **in-memory dict**. If you run uvicorn with multiple worker processes:

```bash
# This would break token validation
uvicorn app.main:app --workers 4
```

A token created in worker 1 is invisible to worker 2. The subprocess's API call could land on a different worker and get a 401.

**Your current setup is fine** — the Dockerfile uses `uvicorn` with no `--workers` flag (defaults to 1). Just don't add multiple workers without replacing the token store first.

If you ever need to scale, the fix is straightforward: swap `proxy_token_store` for a Redis-backed store, or use a single-process async server with multiple async tasks (which is what you have now and handles concurrent requests fine).

    21→_SKIP_RESPONSE_HEADERS = {"transfer-encoding", "content-encoding", "connection"}

    24→class ProxyTokenStore:
    25→    def __init__(self, ttl_seconds: int = 600):
    26→        self._ttl = ttl_seconds
    27→        self._tokens: dict[str, datetime] = {}
    28→
    29→    def create_token(self) -> str:
    30→        token = str(uuid.uuid4())
    31→        self._tokens[token] = datetime.now(timezone.utc) + timedelta(seconds=self._ttl)
    32→        return token
    33→
    34→    def validate_token(self, token: str) -> bool:
    35→        # Tokens are intentionally multi-use: Claude Code makes many API calls
    36→        # per session, all protected by the same UUID. The token is valid for
    37→        # the session TTL (600s safety net) but is explicitly revoked by
    38→        # proxy_token_store.revoke_token() in agent.py's finally block when
    39→        # the Claude Code subprocess exits. Do not make single-use.
    40→        expiry = self._tokens.get(token)
    41→        if expiry is None:
    42→            return False
    43→        if datetime.now(timezone.utc) > expiry:
    44→            self._tokens.pop(token, None)
    45→            return False
    46→        return True
    47→
    48→    def revoke_token(self, token: str) -> None:
    49→        self._tokens.pop(token, None)
    50→
    51→    def cleanup_expired(self) -> int:
    52→        now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
    53→        expired = [t for t, exp in self._tokens.items() if now > exp]
    54→        for t in expired:
    55→            del self._tokens[t]
    56→        return len(expired)

    64→def get_http_client() -> httpx.AsyncClient:

   105→            content=body,
   106→            params=dict(request.query_params),

   113→        }
   114→
   115→        async def body_generator():
   116→            try:
   117→                async for chunk in upstream_resp.aiter_bytes():
   118→                    yield chunk
   119→            finally:
   120→                await upstream_resp.aclose()
   121→
   122→        return StreamingResponse(
   123→            body_generator(),
   124→            status_code=upstream_resp.status_code,
   125→            headers=response_headers,
   126→        )

> AGENT

Found the root cause.

**Root cause: Port mismatch.**

The Makefile runs uvicorn locally on **port 8000**:
```
poetry run uvicorn app.main:app --reload --port 8000
```

But `PROXY_BASE_URL` defaults to `http://127.0.0.1:10000` (the Docker/Render port).

So the Claude Code subprocess tries to reach the proxy at port 10000 — nothing is listening there — and hangs waiting for a connection that never comes.

**Fix: add `PROXY_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8000` to your local `.env`.**

Your `.env` already has `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` set. Just add this line alongside it:

```
PROXY_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8000
```

The `10000` default is correct for the Docker container (Render deployment). Locally you need `8000` to match the Makefile.

No code change needed — the env var was designed to be configurable for exactly this reason.

> DEVELOPER

Error: Failed to authenticate. API Error: 401 {"type":"error","error":{"type":"authentication_error","message":"OAuth authentication is currently not supported."},"request_id":"req_011CYM4FB872P9uZWA22tW4u"}

how to get around with it

> AGENT

**Root cause:** Our proxy forwards `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` to `api.anthropic.com` as a Bearer token, but that API only accepts API keys — OAuth tokens are handled differently by the Claude Code CLI internally.

You have two paths forward:

---

## Option 1: Switch to `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (recommended)

Replace OAuth with a regular API key. The proxy was designed for this — it swaps the session UUID for a real API key before forwarding to `api.anthropic.com`.

In your `.env`:
```
ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=sk-ant-...   # your key from console.anthropic.com
```

Then change the proxy to forward with the API key instead of the OAuth token. Two small edits needed — want me to make them?

---

## Option 2: Revert the proxy

Looking at your `agent.py`, the subprocess already has strict tool restrictions:

```python
allowed_tools=["mcp__duckdb__execute_sql"],
```

With only the SQL tool available, Claude Code **cannot** run bash, execute Python, or read environment variables — the threat the proxy was built to block doesn't apply here. The proxy adds complexity without security benefit in this specific setup.

Reverting means removing the `env=` injection from `ClaudeAgentOptions` and leaving the subprocess to authenticate via `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` as it did before.

---

Which fits your situation better — do you have an API key available, or do you want to revert?

    70→```

    80→
    81→> `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (or `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`) and `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` are only needed for the AI agent. The SQL playground works without them, but both require the backend running.
    82→
    83→#### Langfuse (optional)
    84→
    85→To enable agent tracing with [Langfuse](https://langfuse.com/), add these to `backend/.env`:
    86→
    87→```
    88→LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY=pk-lf-...
    89→LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY=sk-lf-...
    90→LANGFUSE_BASE_URL=https://cloud.langfuse.com   # optional, defaults to cloud
    91→```
    92→
    93→When configured, every agent conversation is traced (LLM turns, tool calls, SQL execution) and a **Langfuse Traces** button appears in the agent panel header linking to your dashboard. When not configured, tracing is disabled with zero overhead.
    94→
    95→### Development
    96→
    97→Start both the frontend and backend:
    98→
    99→```bash
   100→make dev
   101→```
   102→
   103→Or run them separately:
   104→
   105→```bash
   106→make frontend   # http://localhost:5173
   107→make backend    # http://localhost:8000
   108→```
   109→
   110→Open http://localhost:5173 to use the app. The Vite dev server proxies `/api` requests to the backend automatically.
   111→
   112→## Production Build and Deployment
   113→
   114→The project ships as a single Docker image that bundles the React frontend and FastAPI backend. A multi-stage `Dockerfile` builds the frontend, then copies the output into the backend's static directory.
   115→
   116→### Build and run locally
   117→
   118→```bash
   119→docker build -t duckdb-data-agent .
   120→
   121→# Using an Anthropic API key
   122→docker run -p 10000:10000 \
   123→  -e ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=sk-ant-... \
   124→  duckdb-data-agent
   125→
   126→# Or using a Claude Code OAuth token
   127→docker run -p 10000:10000 \
   128→  -e CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN=[REDACTED] \
   129→  duckdb-data-agent
   130→```
   131→
   132→Add `-e LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY=pk-lf-... -e LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY=sk-lf-...` to either command to enable Langfuse tracing.
   133→
   134→Open http://localhost:10000 to use the app.
   135→
   136→### Deploy to Render
   137→
   138→A `render.yaml` is included for one-click deployment on [Render](https://render.com/):
   139→
   140→1. Push this repo to GitHub.
   141→2. In Render, create a new **Blueprint** and connect the repo.
   142→3. Set either `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` or `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` in the Render dashboard. Optionally set `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` to override the default model (`sonnet`). To enable Langfuse tracing, also set `LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY` and `LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY`.
   143→
   144→Render will build the Docker image and deploy it automatically on every push to `main`.
   145→
   146→## Project Structure
   147→
   148→```
   149→├── frontend/               # React frontend
```

    82→async def proxy_anthropic(path: str, request: Request):

> AGENT

**Root cause:** The Anthropic SDK (used by Claude Code CLI) sends the API key as `x-api-key` header — not `Authorization: Bearer`. Our proxy only checks `Authorization: Bearer`, so it returns 401 "Missing Bearer token" immediately.

The proxy also strips `x-api-key` from incoming requests before it even reads it for validation. Two bugs in one line of logic.

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_016t7uQZ78jR4NhRuRQjj4vR
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/proxy.py",
  "old_string": "@router.api_route(\"/{path:path}\", methods=[\"GET\", \"POST\", \"PUT\", \"DELETE\", \"PATCH\"])\nasync def proxy_anthropic(path: str, request: Request):\n    auth_header = request.headers.get(\"authorization\", \"\")\n    if not auth_header.startswith(\"Bearer \"):\n        raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail=\"Missing Bearer token\")\n\n    session_token = auth_header[7:]\n    if not proxy_token_store.validate_token(session_token):\n        raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail=\"Invalid or expired session token\")\n\n    headers = {\n        k: v for k, v in request.headers.items()\n        if k.lower() not in _SKIP_REQUEST_HEADERS\n    }\n    headers[\"authorization\"] = f\"Bearer {ANTHROPIC_API_KEY}\"",
  "new_string": "@router.api_route(\"/{path:path}\", methods=[\"GET\", \"POST\", \"PUT\", \"DELETE\", \"PATCH\"])\nasync def proxy_anthropic(path: str, request: Request):\n    # Claude Code CLI sends the API key as x-api-key (Anthropic SDK default).\n    # Fall back to Authorization: Bearer for other clients.\n    session_token = (\n        request.headers.get(\"x-api-key\")\n        or request.headers.get(\"authorization\", \"\")[len(\"Bearer \"):]\n        or None\n    )\n    if not session_token or not proxy_token_store.validate_token(session_token):\n        raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail=\"Invalid or expired session token\")\n\n    headers = {\n        k: v for k, v in request.headers.items()\n        if k.lower() not in _SKIP_REQUEST_HEADERS\n    }\n    headers[\"x-api-key\"] = ANTHROPIC_API_KEY"
}
```

28 tests pass. The fix: the Anthropic SDK sends credentials as `x-api-key` (not `Authorization: Bearer`), so the proxy now:

1. Reads the session UUID from `x-api-key` first, falls back to `Authorization: Bearer`
2. Forwards to Anthropic with the real key as `x-api-key`

ssl.SSLError: [SSL: SSLV3_ALERT_BAD_RECORD_MAC] ssl/tls alert bad record mac (_ssl.c:2559)
INFO:     127.0.0.1:65187 - "POST /anthropic/v1/messages?beta=true HTTP/1.1" 400 Bad Request

> AGENT

**Root cause:** `ssl.SSLError: SSLV3_ALERT_BAD_RECORD_MAC` — the shared `httpx.AsyncClient` is reusing a TCP connection that was left in a partial state after a previous stream was cut short (e.g., client disconnected mid-stream). When the next request tries to reuse that corrupted connection, TLS MAC verification fails.

The fix: disable keepalive connection pooling on the proxy client. Each proxy request needs a fresh TLS connection to Anthropic since we're streaming long-lived responses.

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01AEHTWJbMxi6hgFapzUF8Wc
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/proxy.py",
  "old_string": "def get_http_client() -> httpx.AsyncClient:\n    global _http_client\n    if _http_client is None:\n        _http_client = httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=httpx.Timeout(300.0))\n    return _http_client",
  "new_string": "def get_http_client() -> httpx.AsyncClient:\n    global _http_client\n    if _http_client is None:\n        # Disable keepalive pooling: streaming responses that are cut short\n        # (e.g. client disconnects) leave connections in a partial read state.\n        # Reusing such connections causes SSLV3_ALERT_BAD_RECORD_MAC errors.\n        _http_client = httpx.AsyncClient(\n            timeout=httpx.Timeout(300.0),\n            limits=httpx.Limits(max_keepalive_connections=0, max_connections=100),\n        )\n    return _http_client"
}
```

> AGENT

Done. `max_keepalive_connections=0` tells httpx never to return a connection to the pool after use — each proxy request to Anthropic gets a fresh TLS handshake. The `max_connections=100` cap still allows concurrent requests without queueing.

> AGENT

Done. The proxy now always forwards `anthropic-version: 2023-06-01` to Anthropic, regardless of what the subprocess sends.

> DEVELOPER

why anthropic-version is 2023-06-01

> AGENT

`2023-06-01` is the **only released version** of the Anthropic Messages API — it's a stable identifier, not a date that gets incremented with new features. The API has kept this version string since launch.

New capabilities (like extended thinking, prompt caching, etc.) are gated behind **`anthropic-beta`** headers instead of a new version string. That's why the two are separate concepts:

| Header | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `anthropic-version: 2023-06-01` | Required baseline — never changes |
| `anthropic-beta: ...` | Opt-in to specific new features |

We strip `anthropic-beta` from incoming requests (by design — the proxy controls which beta features are enabled, not the subprocess). We strip `anthropic-version` and re-inject `2023-06-01` because it's the only valid value anyway.

If Anthropic ever releases a `2024-xx-xx` or later version, you'd update that one line in `proxy.py`.

> AGENT

Two separate root causes:

1. **`SSL record layer failure`** — the shared `httpx.AsyncClient`'s SSL context becomes corrupted after a bad connection. Even with keepalive disabled, the shared client retains a poisoned SSL state.

2. **`context_management: Extra inputs are not permitted`** — the `?beta=true` query parameter from Claude Code CLI is being forwarded to Anthropic, which rejects it as an unknown field.

Both fixes together: drop the shared client (SSL context isolation) and strip query params (Claude Code internal flags that Anthropic doesn't accept).

     2→import uuid
     3→from datetime import datetime, timedelta, timezone

     5→from fastapi import APIRouter, HTTPException, Request
     6→from fastapi.responses import StreamingResponse
     7→import httpx
     8→
     9→from app.config import ANTHROPIC_API_KEY
    10→
    11→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    12→
    13→ANTHROPIC_UPSTREAM = "https://api.anthropic.com"
    14→
    15→_SKIP_REQUEST_HEADERS = {
    16→    "host", "content-length", "transfer-encoding", "connection",
    17→    "x-api-key",         # client must not override billing identity
    18→    "anthropic-version", # proxy controls API version
    19→    "anthropic-beta",    # proxy controls beta feature access
    20→}
    21→_SKIP_RESPONSE_HEADERS = {"transfer-encoding", "content-encoding", "connection"}
    22→
    23→
    24→class ProxyTokenStore:
    25→    def __init__(self, ttl_seconds: int = 600):
    26→        self._ttl = ttl_seconds
    27→        self._tokens: dict[str, datetime] = {}
    28→
    29→    def create_token(self) -> str:
    30→        token = str(uuid.uuid4())
    31→        self._tokens[token] = datetime.now(timezone.utc) + timedelta(seconds=self._ttl)
    32→        return token
    33→
    34→    def validate_token(self, token: str) -> bool:
    35→        # Tokens are intentionally multi-use: Claude Code makes many API calls
    36→        # per session, all protected by the same UUID. The token is valid for
    37→        # the session TTL (600s safety net) but is explicitly revoked by
    38→        # proxy_token_store.revoke_token() in agent.py's finally block when
    39→        # the Claude Code subprocess exits. Do not make single-use.
    40→        expiry = self._tokens.get(token)
    41→        if expiry is None:
    42→            return False
    43→        if datetime.now(timezone.utc) > expiry:
    44→            self._tokens.pop(token, None)
    45→            return False
    46→        return True
    47→
    48→    def revoke_token(self, token: str) -> None:
    49→        self._tokens.pop(token, None)
    50→
    51→    def cleanup_expired(self) -> int:
    52→        now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
    53→        expired = [t for t, exp in self._tokens.items() if now > exp]
    54→        for t in expired:
    55→            del self._tokens[t]
    56→        return len(expired)
    57→
    58→
    59→proxy_token_store = ProxyTokenStore()
    60→
    61→_http_client: httpx.AsyncClient | None = None
    62→
    63→
    64→def get_http_client() -> httpx.AsyncClient:
    65→    global _http_client
    66→    if _http_client is None:
    67→        # Disable keepalive pooling: streaming responses that are cut short
    68→        # (e.g. client disconnects) leave connections in a partial read state.
    69→        # Reusing such connections causes SSLV3_ALERT_BAD_RECORD_MAC errors.
    70→        _http_client = httpx.AsyncClient(
    71→            timeout=httpx.Timeout(300.0),
    72→            limits=httpx.Limits(max_keepalive_connections=0, max_connections=100),
    73→        )
    74→    return _http_client
    75→
    76→
    77→async def close_http_client() -> None:
    78→    global _http_client
    79→    if _http_client is not None:
    80→        await _http_client.aclose()
    81→        _http_client = None
    82→
    83→
    84→router = APIRouter(prefix="/anthropic")
    85→
    86→
    87→@router.api_route("/{path:path}", methods=["GET", "POST", "PUT", "DELETE", "PATCH"])
    88→async def proxy_anthropic(path: str, request: Request):
    89→    # Claude Code CLI sends the API key as x-api-key (Anthropic SDK default).
    90→    # Fall back to Authorization: Bearer for other clients.
    91→    session_token = (
    92→        request.headers.get("x-api-key")
    93→        or request.headers.get("authorization", "")[len("Bearer "):]
    94→        or None
    95→    )
    96→    if not session_token or not proxy_token_store.validate_token(session_token):
    97→        raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Invalid or expired session token")
    98→
    99→    headers = {
   100→        k: v for k, v in request.headers.items()
   101→        if k.lower() not in _SKIP_REQUEST_HEADERS
   102→    }
   103→    headers["x-api-key"] = ANTHROPIC_API_KEY
   104→    headers["anthropic-version"] = "2023-06-01"
   105→
   106→    body = await request.body()
   107→
   108→    client = get_http_client()
   109→    try:
   110→        upstream_req = client.build_request(
   111→            method=request.method,
   112→            url=f"{ANTHROPIC_UPSTREAM}/{path}",
   113→            headers=headers,
   114→            content=body,
   115→            params=dict(request.query_params),
   116→        )
   117→        upstream_resp = await client.send(upstream_req, stream=True)
   118→
   119→        response_headers = {
   120→            k: v for k, v in upstream_resp.headers.items()
   121→            if k.lower() not in _SKIP_RESPONSE_HEADERS
   122→        }
   123→
   124→        async def body_generator():
   125→            try:
   126→                async for chunk in upstream_resp.aiter_bytes():
   127→                    yield chunk
   128→            finally:
   129→                await upstream_resp.aclose()
   130→
   131→        return StreamingResponse(
   132→            body_generator(),
   133→            status_code=upstream_resp.status_code,
   134→            headers=response_headers,
   135→        )
   136→    except Exception:
   137→        logger.exception("Proxy request to upstream failed: %s %s", request.method, path)
   138→        raise
   139→

    19→async def _cleanup_loop():

    22→        removed = session_manager.cleanup_stale(ttl_seconds=300)
    23→        if removed:

    11→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    12→
    13→ANTHROPIC_UPSTREAM = "https://api.anthropic.com"
    14→
    15→_SKIP_REQUEST_HEADERS = {
    16→    "host", "content-length", "transfer-encoding", "connection",
    17→    "x-api-key",         # client must not override billing identity
    18→    "anthropic-version", # proxy controls API version
    19→    "anthropic-beta",    # proxy controls beta feature access
    20→}
    21→_SKIP_RESPONSE_HEADERS = {"transfer-encoding", "content-encoding", "connection"}
    22→
    23→
    24→class ProxyTokenStore:
    25→    def __init__(self, ttl_seconds: int = 600):
    26→        self._ttl = ttl_seconds
    27→        self._tokens: dict[str, datetime] = {}
    28→
    29→    def create_token(self) -> str:
    30→        token = str(uuid.uuid4())
    31→        self._tokens[token] = datetime.now(timezone.utc) + timedelta(seconds=self._ttl)
    32→        return token
    33→
    34→    def validate_token(self, token: str) -> bool:
    35→        # Tokens are intentionally multi-use: Claude Code makes many API calls
    36→        # per session, all protected by the same UUID. The token is valid for
    37→        # the session TTL (600s safety net) but is explicitly revoked by
    38→        # proxy_token_store.revoke_token() in agent.py's finally block when
    39→        # the Claude Code subprocess exits. Do not make single-use.
    40→        expiry = self._tokens.get(token)
    41→        if expiry is None:
    42→            return False
    43→        if datetime.now(timezone.utc) > expiry:
    44→            self._tokens.pop(token, None)
    45→            return False
    46→        return True
    47→
    48→    def revoke_token(self, token: str) -> None:
    49→        self._tokens.pop(token, None)
    50→
    51→    def cleanup_expired(self) -> int:
    52→        now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
    53→        expired = [t for t, exp in self._tokens.items() if now > exp]
    54→        for t in expired:
    55→            del self._tokens[t]
    56→        return len(expired)
    57→
    58→
    59→proxy_token_store = ProxyTokenStore()
    60→
    61→router = APIRouter(prefix="/anthropic")
    62→
    63→
    64→@router.api_route("/{path:path}", methods=["GET", "POST", "PUT", "DELETE", "PATCH"])
    65→async def proxy_anthropic(path: str, request: Request):
    66→    # Claude Code CLI sends the API key as x-api-key (Anthropic SDK default).
    67→    # Fall back to Authorization: Bearer for other clients.
    68→    session_token = (
    69→        request.headers.get("x-api-key")
    70→        or request.headers.get("authorization", "")[len("Bearer "):]
    71→        or None
    72→    )
    73→    if not session_token or not proxy_token_store.validate_token(session_token):
    74→        raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Invalid or expired session token")
    75→
    76→    headers = {
    77→        k: v for k, v in request.headers.items()
    78→        if k.lower() not in _SKIP_REQUEST_HEADERS
    79→    }
    80→    headers["x-api-key"] = ANTHROPIC_API_KEY
    81→    headers["anthropic-version"] = "2023-06-01"
    82→
    83→    body = await request.body()
    84→
    85→    # Use a fresh client per request: streaming responses that are cut short
    86→    # (e.g. client disconnects) leave connections in a partial read state, and
    87→    # reusing a shared SSL context across those connections causes
    88→    # SSLV3_ALERT_BAD_RECORD_MAC / record layer failure errors.
    89→    # Query params are intentionally NOT forwarded — Claude Code appends
    90→    # internal params (e.g. ?beta=true) that Anthropic rejects as "extra inputs".
    91→    client = httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=httpx.Timeout(300.0))
    92→    try:
    93→        upstream_req = client.build_request(
    94→            method=request.method,
    95→            url=f"{ANTHROPIC_UPSTREAM}/{path}",
    96→            headers=headers,
    97→            content=body,
    98→        )
    99→        upstream_resp = await client.send(upstream_req, stream=True)
   100→
   101→        response_headers = {
   102→            k: v for k, v in upstream_resp.headers.items()
   103→            if k.lower() not in _SKIP_RESPONSE_HEADERS
   104→        }
   105→
   106→        async def body_generator():
   107→            try:
   108→                async for chunk in upstream_resp.aiter_bytes():
   109→                    yield chunk
   110→            finally:
   111→                await upstream_resp.aclose()
   112→                await client.aclose()
   113→
   114→        return StreamingResponse(
   115→            body_generator(),
   116→            status_code=upstream_resp.status_code,
   117→            headers=response_headers,
   118→        )
   119→    except Exception:
   120→        await client.aclose()
   121→        logger.exception("Proxy request to upstream failed: %s %s", request.method, path)
   122→        raise
   123→
```

> AGENT

All 12 tests pass. Here's what was fixed:

**Two changes to `backend/app/proxy.py`:**

1. **SSL context corruption** — Removed the shared `httpx.AsyncClient` entirely. Each request now creates a fresh client, which is closed in `body_generator()`'s `finally` block (normal path) or in the `except` block (error path). This eliminates the `SSLV3_ALERT_BAD_RECORD_MAC` / `record layer failure` errors caused by reusing SSL connections left in a partial read state after a client disconnect.

2. **`?beta=true` → 400 error** — Removed `params=dict(request.query_params)` from `client.build_request(...)`. Claude Code appends internal query params that Anthropic's API rejects as `Extra inputs are not permitted`.

**One change to `backend/app/main.py`:**
- Removed `await proxy_module.close_http_client()` from the lifespan shutdown since there's no longer a shared client to close.

> DEVELOPER

still see this: Error: API Error: 400 {"type":"error","error":{"type":"invalid_request_error","message":"context_management: Extra inputs are not permitted"},"request_id":"req_011CYM5rBj9vhRVPfK5XV4he"}

> AGENT

The error is in the **request body**, not query params. `context_management` is a beta feature field Claude Code sends in the JSON body — but we're stripping the `anthropic-beta` header that tells Anthropic to accept it, so Anthropic sees an unknown field and rejects it.

The fix: stop blocking `anthropic-beta` and let it pass through.

  "new_string": "_SKIP_REQUEST_HEADERS = {\n    \"host\", \"content-length\", \"transfer-encoding\", \"connection\",\n    \"x-api-key\",         # client must not override billing identity\n    \"anthropic-version\", # proxy controls API version\n    # anthropic-beta is intentionally NOT blocked: Claude Code sends beta body\n    # fields (e.g. context_management) alongside the matching beta header, and\n    # Anthropic rejects those fields as \"extra inputs\" if the header is absent.\n}"

> AGENT

All tests pass. 

**Root cause:** Claude Code sends `context_management` in the JSON body (a beta feature) accompanied by an `anthropic-beta` header that signals Anthropic to accept it. The proxy was stripping `anthropic-beta` but passing the body through unchanged — so Anthropic saw the `context_management` field with no beta header to authorize it, and rejected it as `Extra inputs are not permitted`.

**Fix:** Removed `anthropic-beta` from `_SKIP_REQUEST_HEADERS`. The header now passes through transparently, allowing Anthropic to accept whatever beta body fields Claude Code sends.
