```
     1→# DuckDB Data Agent
     2→
     3→> **Note:** This project is built with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/product/claude-code)(using Opus 4.6 and [superpowers](https://github.com/obra/superpowers)) by more than 95% and human-reviewed by author.
     4→
     5→[![Deploy to Render](https://render.com/images/deploy-to-render-button.svg)](https://render.com/deploy)
     6→
     7→An AI-powered data analysis agent with a built-in SQL playground. Upload data files (CSV, JSON, Parquet, Excel) and ask questions in plain English, or switch to the SQL editor for direct queries — powered by [DuckDB](https://duckdb.org/) on a lightweight [FastAPI](https://fastapi.tiangolo.com/) backend with a React frontend. The app opens in Agent Mode by default so you can start analyzing data immediately.
     8→
     9→Each browser tab gets its own isolated, in-memory DuckDB session — uploaded data and query state are fully isolated between users and tabs, with idle sessions automatically cleaned up after 5 minutes of inactivity.
    10→
    11→## Features
    12→
    13→### General
    14→
    15→- **Per-user DuckDB sessions** — Each browser tab gets its own isolated in-memory DuckDB instance, identified by a `X-Session-ID` header generated client-side; data and state are never shared between users or tabs; idle sessions are automatically cleaned up after 5 minutes
    16→- **DuckDB SQL engine** — Fast, in-process analytical database on the backend
    17→- **Multi-format file upload** — Drag-and-drop or click to import CSV, JSON, Parquet, and Excel (.xlsx) files (default limit: 500 MB, configurable via `MAX_TOTAL_SIZE_BYTES` env var) with automatic schema detection; Excel workbooks with multiple sheets create one table per sheet; duplicate filename detection prevents accidental overwrites; the upload UI appears when no tables are loaded, and files can also be added via the sidebar upload button
    18→- **Sample dataset** — One-click load of the Titanic dataset to get started quickly
    19→- **Table sidebar** — Collapsible panel to browse tables, inspect columns, and view types
    20→- **Dark / light mode** — Toggle between dark and light themes with the sun/moon button in the header; respects your OS preference on first visit and remembers your choice across sessions
    21→- **Internationalization (i18n)** — Switch between English and Traditional Chinese with the EN/中 toggle in the header; auto-detects your OS language on first visit and remembers your choice across sessions
    22→
    23→### Agent Mode (default mode)
    24→
    25→- **Natural language queries** — Ask questions about your data in plain English; the agent writes and executes SQL for you
    26→- **Streaming responses** — Real-time token streaming powered by Claude via the [Anthropic Agent SDK](https://github.com/anthropics/anthropic-sdk-python)
    27→- **Visible reasoning** — Collapsible thinking block shows the agent's intermediate steps and SQL queries
    28→- **Inline results** — Query results rendered inline within the conversation
    29→- **Edit & delete messages** — Hover over any user message to edit or delete it; editing re-sends the modified query with prior conversation as context, deleting rewinds the conversation to that point
    30→- **Privacy-conscious** — Requires an Anthropic API key or Claude Code OAuth token stored in a server-side `.env` file; your data and credentials are never sent anywhere besides the Anthropic API
    31→- **Langfuse observability** (optional) — Built-in [Langfuse](https://langfuse.com/) tracing for monitoring agent interactions, with a one-click dashboard link in the UI
    32→
    33→### Editor Mode
    34→
    35→- **SQL query editor** — Write and execute queries with Ctrl/Cmd+Enter
    36→- **Interactive results** — Sortable columns, per-column filters, and global search across results
    37→- **EXPLAIN support** — Markdown-rendered output for `EXPLAIN` and `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` queries
    38→
    39→## Getting Started
    40→
    41→### Prerequisites
    42→
    43→- [Node.js](https://nodejs.org/) 20+
    44→- [Python](https://www.python.org/) 3.12+
    45→- [Poetry](https://python-poetry.org/)
    46→
    47→### Installation
    48→
    49→```bash
    50→make install
    51→```
    52→
    53→Or install frontend and backend separately:
    54→
    55→```bash
    56→cd frontend && npm install
    57→cd backend && poetry install
    58→```
    59→
    60→### Configuration
    61→
    62→Copy the example environment file and add your credentials:
    63→
    64→```bash
    65→cp backend/.env.example backend/.env
    66→```
    67→
    68→Edit `backend/.env` and set your credentials. You can authenticate with either an Anthropic API key **or** a Claude Code OAuth token:
    69→
    70→```
    71→ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=sk-ant-...
    72→ANTHROPIC_MODEL=sonnet              # optional, defaults to sonnet
    73→MAX_TOTAL_SIZE_BYTES=524288000      # optional, max upload size in bytes (default: 500 MB)
    74→```
    75→
    76→> `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` are only needed for the AI agent. The SQL playground works without them, but both require the backend running.
    77→
    78→#### Langfuse (optional)
    79→
    80→To enable agent tracing with [Langfuse](https://langfuse.com/), add these to `backend/.env`:
    81→
    82→```
    83→LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY=pk-lf-...
    84→LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY=sk-lf-...
    85→LANGFUSE_BASE_URL=https://cloud.langfuse.com   # optional, defaults to cloud
    86→```
    87→
    88→When configured, every agent conversation is traced (LLM turns, tool calls, SQL execution) and a **Langfuse Traces** button appears in the agent panel header linking to your dashboard. When not configured, tracing is disabled with zero overhead.
    89→
    90→### Development
    91→
    92→Start both the frontend and backend:
    93→
    94→```bash
    95→make dev
    96→```
    97→
    98→Or run them separately:
    99→
   100→```bash
   101→make frontend   # http://localhost:5173
   102→make backend    # http://localhost:8000
   103→```
   104→
   105→Open http://localhost:5173 to use the app. The Vite dev server proxies `/api` requests to the backend automatically.
   106→
   107→## Production Build and Deployment
   108→
   109→The project ships as a single Docker image that bundles the React frontend and FastAPI backend. A multi-stage `Dockerfile` builds the frontend, then copies the output into the backend's static directory.
   110→
   111→### Build and run locally
   112→
   113→```bash
   114→docker build -t duckdb-data-agent .
   115→
   116→docker run -p 10000:10000 \
   117→  -e ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=sk-ant-... \
   118→  duckdb-data-agent
   119→```
   120→
   121→Add `-e LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY=pk-lf-... -e LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY=sk-lf-...` to either command to enable Langfuse tracing.
   122→
   123→Open http://localhost:10000 to use the app.
   124→
   125→### Deploy to Render
   126→
   127→A `render.yaml` is included for one-click deployment on [Render](https://render.com/):
   128→
   129→1. Push this repo to GitHub.
   130→2. In Render, create a new **Blueprint** and connect the repo.
   131→3. Set `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in the Render dashboard. Optionally set `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` to override the default model (`sonnet`). To enable Langfuse tracing, also set `LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY` and `LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY`.
   132→
   133→Render will build the Docker image and deploy it automatically on every push to `main`.
   134→
   135→## Project Structure
   136→
   137→```
   138→├── frontend/               # React frontend
   139→│   ├── src/
   140→│   │   ├── components/     #   UI components (editor, results, sidebar, chat)
   141→│   │   ├── contexts/       #   React context providers (theme, language, agent, config, session)
   142→│   │   ├── hooks/          #   Custom hooks (useTheme, useTranslation, useAgent, useConfig)
   143→│   │   ├── agent/          #   Agent service (SSE event handling, session ID injection)
   144→│   │   ├── i18n/           #   Translation files (en.json, zh-TW.json)
   145→│   │   └── types.ts        #   Shared TypeScript interfaces
   146→│   ├── index.html          #   HTML entry point
   147→│   ├── package.json        #   npm config
   148→│   └── vite.config.ts      #   Vite bundler config
   149→├── backend/                # FastAPI backend
   150→│   └── app/
   151→│       ├── main.py         #   App setup, CORS, and background session cleanup loop
   152→│       ├── config.py       #   Environment variables (API key, model, upload limits)
   153→│       ├── database.py     #   DuckDB connection, query execution, and per-user SessionManager
   154→│       ├── agent.py        #   Agent loop & SSE streaming
   155→│       ├── tracing.py      #   Langfuse client wrapper & initialization
   156→│       ├── tools.py        #   Agent SDK tool definitions (execute_sql)
   157→│       ├── data/           #   Sample datasets (titanic.csv)
   158→│       └── routes/         #   API endpoints (tables, query, chat, config, langfuse status, heartbeat)
   159→├── Dockerfile              # Multi-stage production build
   160→├── render.yaml             # Render deployment config
   161→└── Makefile                # Dev commands (install, dev, clean)
   162→```
   163→
   164→## Tech Stack
   165→
   166→**Frontend**
   167→- [React](https://react.dev/) 18 + [TypeScript](https://www.typescriptlang.org/)
   168→- [Vite](https://vite.dev/)
   169→
   170→**Backend**
   171→- [FastAPI](https://fastapi.tiangolo.com/) + [Uvicorn](https://www.uvicorn.org/)
   172→- [DuckDB](https://duckdb.org/) (Python)
   173→- [Anthropic Agent SDK](https://github.com/anthropics/anthropic-sdk-python)
   174→- [Langfuse](https://langfuse.com/) (optional, for observability)
   175→
   176→## License
   177→
   178→[MIT](LICENSE.txt)
   179→
```

     1→import json
     2→import logging
     3→from typing import AsyncIterator
     4→
     5→from claude_agent_sdk import (
     6→    ClaudeSDKClient,
     7→    ClaudeAgentOptions,
     8→    AssistantMessage,
     9→    UserMessage,
    10→    ResultMessage,
    11→    ToolUseBlock,
    12→    ToolResultBlock,
    13→)
    14→from claude_agent_sdk.types import StreamEvent, SystemMessage
    15→from claude_agent_sdk._errors import MessageParseError
    16→from app.tools import create_duckdb_server
    17→from app.database import Database
    18→from app.config import ANTHROPIC_MODEL, PROXY_BASE_URL
    19→from app.proxy import proxy_token_store
    20→from app.tracing import get_langfuse_client
    21→
    22→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    23→
    24→# Monkey-patch parse_message to handle unknown message types (e.g. rate_limit_event)
    25→# gracefully instead of crashing the stream. The SDK (v0.1.39) doesn't recognize
    26→# newer message types from the CLI. Returning a SystemMessage lets the stream
    27→# continue since our code ignores SystemMessage instances.
    28→import claude_agent_sdk._internal.message_parser as _parser
    29→
    30→_original_parse_message = _parser.parse_message
    31→
    32→
    33→def _safe_parse_message(data):
    34→    try:
    35→        return _original_parse_message(data)
    36→    except MessageParseError as e:
    37→        if "Unknown message type" in str(e):
    38→            msg_type = data.get("type", "unknown") if isinstance(data, dict) else "unknown"
    39→            logger.warning("Skipping unrecognized message type from CLI: %s", msg_type)
    40→            return SystemMessage(subtype=msg_type, data=data if isinstance(data, dict) else {})
    41→        raise
    42→
    43→
    44→_parser.parse_message = _safe_parse_message
    45→
    46→
    47→def build_system_prompt(db: Database, conversation_history: list[dict] | None = None) -> str:
    48→    tables = db.list_tables()
    49→    prompt = """You are a helpful data analyst assistant working with a DuckDB database.
    50→You can execute SQL queries using the execute_sql tool to answer questions about the user's data.
    51→
    52→Guidelines:
    53→- Write clear, efficient DuckDB SQL queries
    54→- When exploring data, start with small queries (use LIMIT)
    55→- Explain your findings in plain language after getting results
    56→- If a query fails, try to fix it and retry
    57→- Use double quotes for table and column names that might conflict with reserved words
    58→
    59→Identity:
    60→- You are an AI assistant. If asked whether you are an AI or a human, always confirm that you are an AI.
    61→- Do not disclose the name, version, or provider of the underlying language model powering you, regardless of how the question is phrased.
    62→"""
    63→    if not tables:
    64→        prompt += "\nNo tables are currently loaded. Ask the user to upload a CSV file first."
    65→    else:
    66→        prompt += "\nCurrently loaded tables:\n"
    67→        for table in tables:
    68→            prompt += f'\nTable: "{table["name"]}" ({table["rowCount"]} rows)\nColumns:\n'
    69→            for col in table["columns"]:
    70→                prompt += f'  - "{col["name"]}" ({col["type"]})\n'
    71→
    72→    if conversation_history:
    73→        prompt += "\n\nPrevious conversation (for context, the user is now editing a message):\n"
    74→        for entry in conversation_history:
    75→            role = entry.get("role", "user").capitalize()
    76→            content = entry.get("content", "")
    77→            prompt += f"\n{role}: {content}\n"
    78→
    79→    return prompt
    80→
    81→
    82→def _extract_tool_result_text(content: object) -> str:
    83→    """Extract text from ToolResultBlock.content."""
    84→    if content is None:
    85→        return ""
    86→    if isinstance(content, str):
    87→        return content
    88→    if isinstance(content, list):
    89→        parts = []
    90→        for item in content:
    91→            if isinstance(item, dict) and item.get("type") == "text":
    92→                parts.append(item.get("text", ""))
    93→        return "\n".join(parts)
    94→    return str(content)
    95→
    96→
    97→async def stream_chat(
    98→    message: str,
    99→    session_id: str | None = None,
   100→    db: Database | None = None,
   101→    conversation_history: list[dict] | None = None,
   102→    langfuse_session_id: str | None = None,
   103→) -> AsyncIterator[str]:
   104→    """Stream agent chat responses as SSE events."""
   105→    if db is None:
   106→        raise ValueError("db must be provided")
   107→    duckdb_server = create_duckdb_server(db)
   108→
   109→    logger.info("Using model: %s", ANTHROPIC_MODEL)
   110→
   111→    # Collect stderr from the CLI subprocess for debugging
   112→    stderr_lines: list[str] = []
   113→
   114→    # Use the --resume flag to continue an existing session
   115→    session_token=[REDACTED].create_token()
   116→    options = ClaudeAgentOptions(
   117→        model=ANTHROPIC_MODEL,
   118→        system_prompt=build_system_prompt(db, conversation_history),
   119→        mcp_servers={"duckdb": duckdb_server},
   120→        allowed_tools=["mcp__duckdb__execute_sql"],
   121→        permission_mode="bypassPermissions",
   122→        max_turns=20,
   123→        include_partial_messages=True,
   124→        stderr=lambda line: stderr_lines.append(line),
   125→        env={
   126→            "ANTHROPIC_API_KEY": session_token,
   127→            "ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL": f"{PROXY_BASE_URL}/anthropic",
   128→        },
   129→        **({"resume": session_id} if session_id else {}),
   130→    )
   131→
   132→    client = ClaudeSDKClient(options=options)
   133→    # Will be set from the CLI's ResultMessage; use the passed-in value until then
   134→    actual_session_id = session_id
   135→
   136→    # --- Langfuse OTel tracing setup (conditional) ---
   137→    # Deferred: session_id is set after the CLI returns it in ResultMessage
   138→    langfuse = get_langfuse_client()
   139→    observation_ctx = None
   140→    propagate_ctx = None
   141→    if langfuse:
   142→        try:
   143→            trace_input: dict = {"message": message[:500]}
   144→            if conversation_history:
   145→                trace_input["conversation_history"] = conversation_history
   146→            observation_ctx = langfuse.start_as_current_observation(
   147→                name="agent-chat",
   148→                input=trace_input,
   149→                metadata={"model": ANTHROPIC_MODEL},
   150→            )
   151→            observation_ctx.__enter__()
   152→
   153→            # Propagate the stable conversation session_id for child spans
   154→            effective_langfuse_session_id = langfuse_session_id or session_id
   155→            if effective_langfuse_session_id:
   156→                from langfuse import propagate_attributes
   157→                propagate_ctx = propagate_attributes(session_id=effective_langfuse_session_id)
   158→                propagate_ctx.__enter__()
   159→        except Exception as e:
   160→            logger.debug("Failed to set up Langfuse tracing context: %s", e)
   161→            observation_ctx = None
   162→
   163→    try:
   164→        await client.connect()
   165→        await client.query(message, session_id=session_id or "default")
   166→
   167→        has_tool_calls = False
   168→        has_thinking = False
   169→        sql_result_ids: set[str] = set()
   170→        tool_names: dict[str, str] = {}
   171→
   172→        async for msg in client.receive_response():
   173→            if isinstance(msg, StreamEvent):
   174→                event = msg.event
   175→                event_type = event.get("type", "")
   176→
   177→                if event_type == "content_block_delta":
   178→                    delta = event.get("delta", {})
   179→                    delta_type = delta.get("type", "")
   180→                    if delta_type == "thinking_delta":
   181→                        text = delta.get("thinking", "")
   182→                        if text:
   183→                            yield f"event: thinking\ndata: {json.dumps({'text': text})}\n\n"
   184→                    elif delta_type == "text_delta":
   185→                        text = delta.get("text", "")
   186→                        event_name = "thinking" if not has_tool_calls else "answer"
   187→                        yield f"event: {event_name}\ndata: {json.dumps({'text': text})}\n\n"
   188→
   189→                elif event_type == "content_block_start":
   190→                    block = event.get("content_block", {})
   191→                    block_type = block.get("type")
   192→                    if block_type == "thinking":
   193→                        has_thinking = True
   194→                    elif block_type == "text":
   195→                        if has_thinking:
   196→                            yield f"event: thinking_done\ndata: {json.dumps({})}\n\n"
   197→                    elif block_type == "tool_use":
   198→                        has_thinking = False
   199→                        has_tool_calls = True
   200→
   201→            elif isinstance(msg, AssistantMessage):
   202→                for block in msg.content:
   203→                    if isinstance(block, ToolUseBlock):
   204→                        has_tool_calls = True
   205→                        tool_name = getattr(block, "name", "") or ""
   206→                        tool_names[block.id] = tool_name
   207→                        sql = block.input.get("sql", "")
   208→                        command = block.input.get("command", "")
   209→
   210→                        # Emit tool_call for ALL tool types
   211→                        tool_call_data: dict = {"id": block.id, "name": tool_name}
   212→                        if sql:
   213→                            tool_call_data["sql"] = sql
   214→                        if command:
   215→                            tool_call_data["command"] = command
   216→                        if not sql and not command:
   217→                            tool_call_data["input"] = block.input
   218→                        yield f"event: tool_call\ndata: {json.dumps(tool_call_data, default=str)}\n\n"
   219→
   220→                        # For SQL tools, execute query for structured results
   221→                        if sql:
   222→                            sql_result_ids.add(block.id)
   223→                            try:
   224→                                result = db.execute_query(sql)
   225→                                truncated = result["rows"][:100]
   226→                                yield f"event: tool_result\ndata: {json.dumps({'id': block.id, 'name': tool_name, 'sql': sql, 'columns': result['columns'], 'rows': truncated, 'rowCount': result['rowCount']}, default=str)}\n\n"
   227→                            except Exception as e:
   228→                                yield f"event: tool_result\ndata: {json.dumps({'id': block.id, 'name': tool_name, 'sql': sql, 'error': str(e)})}\n\n"
   229→
   230→            elif isinstance(msg, UserMessage):
   231→                # Capture tool results from the SDK for non-SQL tools
   232→                content = msg.content
   233→                if isinstance(content, list):
   234→                    for block in content:
   235→                        if isinstance(block, ToolResultBlock):
   236→                            if block.tool_use_id in sql_result_ids:
   237→                                continue
   238→                            output = _extract_tool_result_text(block.content)
   239→                            name = tool_names.get(block.tool_use_id, "")
   240→                            result_data: dict = {
   241→                                "id": block.tool_use_id,
   242→                                "name": name,
   243→                                "output": output,
   244→                            }
   245→                            if block.is_error:
   246→                                result_data["error"] = output
   247→                            yield f"event: tool_result\ndata: {json.dumps(result_data, default=str)}\n\n"
   248→
   249→            elif isinstance(msg, ResultMessage):
   250→                # Use the CLI's session_id so --resume can find it
   251→                actual_session_id = msg.session_id or actual_session_id
   252→                if msg.is_error and msg.result:
   253→                    yield f"event: error\ndata: {json.dumps({'message': msg.result})}\n\n"
   254→                yield f"event: done\ndata: {json.dumps({'session_id': actual_session_id})}\n\n"
   255→
   256→    except Exception as e:
   257→        error_msg = str(e)
   258→        if stderr_lines:
   259→            error_msg += f" | CLI stderr: {' '.join(stderr_lines[-5:])}"
   260→        logger.error("Agent error: %s", error_msg)
   261→        yield f"event: error\ndata: {json.dumps({'message': error_msg})}\n\n"
   262→    finally:
   263→        proxy_token_store.revoke_token(session_token)
   264→        # Update trace with the CLI's session_id so Langfuse session matches
   265→        if langfuse and observation_ctx:
   266→            try:
   267→                trace_session_id = langfuse_session_id or actual_session_id
   268→                langfuse.update_current_trace(
   269→                    session_id=trace_session_id,
   270→                    output={"session_id": actual_session_id},
   271→                )
   272→                if propagate_ctx:
   273→                    propagate_ctx.__exit__(None, None, None)
   274→                observation_ctx.__exit__(None, None, None)
   275→            except Exception as e:
   276→                logger.debug("Failed to finalize Langfuse trace: %s", e)
   277→            try:
   278→                langfuse.flush()
   279→            except Exception as e:
   280→                logger.debug("Failed to flush Langfuse: %s", e)
   281→
   282→        try:
   283→            await client.disconnect()
   284→        except Exception:
   285→            pass
   286→

  "old_string": "### Agent Mode (default mode)\n\n- **Natural language queries** — Ask questions about your data in plain English; the agent writes and executes SQL for you\n- **Streaming responses** — Real-time token streaming powered by Claude via the [Anthropic Agent SDK](https://github.com/anthropics/anthropic-sdk-python)\n- **Visible reasoning** — Collapsible thinking block shows the agent's intermediate steps and SQL queries\n- **Inline results** — Query results rendered inline within the conversation\n- **Edit & delete messages** — Hover over any user message to edit or delete it; editing re-sends the modified query with prior conversation as context, deleting rewinds the conversation to that point\n- **Privacy-conscious** — Requires an Anthropic API key or Claude Code OAuth token stored in a server-side `.env` file; your data and credentials are never sent anywhere besides the Anthropic API\n- **Langfuse observability** (optional) — Built-in [Langfuse](https://langfuse.com/) tracing for monitoring agent interactions, with a one-click dashboard link in the UI",
  "new_string": "### Agent Mode (default mode)\n\n- **Natural language queries** — Ask questions about your data in plain English; the agent writes and executes SQL for you\n- **Streaming responses** — Real-time token streaming powered by Claude via the [Anthropic Agent SDK](https://github.com/anthropics/anthropic-sdk-python)\n- **Visible reasoning** — Collapsible thinking block shows the agent's intermediate steps and SQL queries\n- **Inline results** — Query results rendered inline within the conversation\n- **Edit & delete messages** — Hover over any user message to edit or delete it; editing re-sends the modified query with prior conversation as context, deleting rewinds the conversation to that point\n- **Credential proxy** — The backend runs a built-in Anthropic API reverse proxy; each agent session receives a short-lived UUID token instead of the real API key, so the Claude Code subprocess never has access to `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`; tokens are revoked immediately when the session ends (see [Security](#security))\n- **Privacy-conscious** — Requires an Anthropic API key stored in a server-side `.env` file; your data and credentials are never sent anywhere besides the Anthropic API\n- **Langfuse observability** (optional) — Built-in [Langfuse](https://langfuse.com/) tracing for monitoring agent interactions, with a one-click dashboard link in the UI"

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/README.md",
  "old_string": "## Project Structure",
  "new_string": "## Security\n\n### Credential Proxy\n\nWhen the agent runs, the backend spawns a Claude Code subprocess via the Anthropic Agent SDK. A naive approach would pass `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` directly into that subprocess's environment — but any tool or shell command the agent executes could then read and exfiltrate the key.\n\nInstead, the backend runs a built-in reverse proxy at `/anthropic` that sits between Claude Code and `api.anthropic.com`:\n\n```\nClaude Code subprocess\n  → ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:{PORT}/anthropic\n  → ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=<short-lived UUID token>\n        ↓\nFastAPI proxy (/anthropic/{path})\n  → validates UUID token\n  → swaps it for the real ANTHROPIC_API_KEY\n  → forwards request to api.anthropic.com\n```\n\n**How it works:**\n\n1. Before each agent session, the backend mints a random UUID token with a 10-minute TTL.\n2. The token is injected into the subprocess environment as `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`; the real key is never exposed.\n3. The proxy validates every inbound request against the token store and substitutes the real key before forwarding upstream.\n4. When the session ends, the token is explicitly revoked in a `finally` block, regardless of success or error.\n5. A background task runs every 60 seconds to sweep any tokens that outlived their TTL.\n\nThe subprocess only ever holds a single-session UUID. Even if a tool call reads the environment, all it gets is a temporary token scoped to that conversation.\n\n## Project Structure"
}
```

     1→# DuckDB Data Agent
     2→
     3→> **Note:** This project is built with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/product/claude-code)(using Opus 4.6 and [superpowers](https://github.com/obra/superpowers)) by more than 95% and human-reviewed by author.

     5→[![Deploy to Render](https://render.com/images/deploy-to-render-button.svg)](https://render.com/deploy)
     6→
     7→An AI-powered data analysis agent with a built-in SQL playground. Upload data files (CSV, JSON, Parquet, Excel) and ask questions in plain English, or switch to the SQL editor for direct queries — powered by [DuckDB](https://duckdb.org/) on a lightweight [FastAPI](https://fastapi.tiangolo.com/) backend with a React frontend. The app opens in Agent Mode by default so you can start analyzing data immediately.
     8→
     9→Each browser tab gets its own isolated, in-memory DuckDB session — uploaded data and query state are fully isolated between users and tabs, with idle sessions automatically cleaned up after 5 minutes of inactivity.
    10→
    11→## Features
    12→
    13→### General
    14→
    15→- **Per-user DuckDB sessions** — Each browser tab gets its own isolated in-memory DuckDB instance, identified by a `X-Session-ID` header generated client-side; data and state are never shared between users or tabs; idle sessions are automatically cleaned up after 5 minutes
    16→- **DuckDB SQL engine** — Fast, in-process analytical database on the backend
    17→- **Multi-format file upload** — Drag-and-drop or click to import CSV, JSON, Parquet, and Excel (.xlsx) files (default limit: 500 MB, configurable via `MAX_TOTAL_SIZE_BYTES` env var) with automatic schema detection; Excel workbooks with multiple sheets create one table per sheet; duplicate filename detection prevents accidental overwrites; the upload UI appears when no tables are loaded, and files can also be added via the sidebar upload button
    18→- **Sample dataset** — One-click load of the Titanic dataset to get started quickly
    19→- **Table sidebar** — Collapsible panel to browse tables, inspect columns, and view types
    20→- **Dark / light mode** — Toggle between dark and light themes with the sun/moon button in the header; respects your OS preference on first visit and remembers your choice across sessions
    21→- **Internationalization (i18n)** — Switch between English and Traditional Chinese with the EN/中 toggle in the header; auto-detects your OS language on first visit and remembers your choice across sessions
    22→
    23→### Agent Mode (default mode)
    24→
    25→- **Natural language queries** — Ask questions about your data in plain English; the agent writes and executes SQL for you
    26→- **Streaming responses** — Real-time token streaming powered by Claude via the [Anthropic Agent SDK](https://github.com/anthropics/anthropic-sdk-python)
    27→- **Visible reasoning** — Collapsible thinking block shows the agent's intermediate steps and SQL queries
    28→- **Inline results** — Query results rendered inline within the conversation
    29→- **Edit & delete messages** — Hover over any user message to edit or delete it; editing re-sends the modified query with prior conversation as context, deleting rewinds the conversation to that point
    30→- **Credential proxy** — The backend runs a built-in Anthropic API reverse proxy; each agent session receives a short-lived UUID token instead of the real API key, so the Claude Code subprocess never has access to `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`; tokens are revoked immediately when the session ends (see [Security](#security))
    31→- **Privacy-conscious** — Requires an Anthropic API key stored in a server-side `.env` file; your data and credentials are never sent anywhere besides the Anthropic API
    32→- **Langfuse observability** (optional) — Built-in [Langfuse](https://langfuse.com/) tracing for monitoring agent interactions, with a one-click dashboard link in the UI
    33→
    34→### Editor Mode
    35→
    36→- **SQL query editor** — Write and execute queries with Ctrl/Cmd+Enter
    37→- **Interactive results** — Sortable columns, per-column filters, and global search across results
    38→- **EXPLAIN support** — Markdown-rendered output for `EXPLAIN` and `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` queries
    39→
    40→## Getting Started
    41→
    42→### Prerequisites
    43→
    44→- [Node.js](https://nodejs.org/) 20+
    45→- [Python](https://www.python.org/) 3.12+
    46→- [Poetry](https://python-poetry.org/)
    47→
    48→### Installation
    49→
    50→```bash
    51→make install
    52→```
    53→
    54→Or install frontend and backend separately:
    55→
    56→```bash
    57→cd frontend && npm install
    58→cd backend && poetry install
    59→```
    60→
    61→### Configuration
    62→
    63→Copy the example environment file and add your credentials:
    64→
    65→```bash
    66→cp backend/.env.example backend/.env
    67→```
    68→
    69→Edit `backend/.env` and set your Anthropic API key:
    70→
    71→```
    72→ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=sk-ant-...
    73→ANTHROPIC_MODEL=sonnet              # optional, defaults to sonnet
    74→MAX_TOTAL_SIZE_BYTES=524288000      # optional, max upload size in bytes (default: 500 MB)
    75→```
    76→
    77→> `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` are only needed for the AI agent. The SQL playground works without them, but both require the backend running.
    78→
    79→#### Langfuse (optional)
    80→
    81→To enable agent tracing with [Langfuse](https://langfuse.com/), add these to `backend/.env`:
    82→
    83→```
    84→LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY=pk-lf-...
    85→LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY=sk-lf-...
    86→LANGFUSE_BASE_URL=https://cloud.langfuse.com   # optional, defaults to cloud
    87→```
    88→
    89→When configured, every agent conversation is traced (LLM turns, tool calls, SQL execution) and a **Langfuse Traces** button appears in the agent panel header linking to your dashboard. When not configured, tracing is disabled with zero overhead.
    90→
    91→### Development
    92→
    93→Start both the frontend and backend:
    94→
    95→```bash
    96→make dev
    97→```
    98→
    99→Or run them separately:
   100→
   101→```bash
   102→make frontend   # http://localhost:5173
   103→make backend    # http://localhost:8000
   104→```
   105→
   106→Open http://localhost:5173 to use the app. The Vite dev server proxies `/api` requests to the backend automatically.
   107→
   108→## Production Build and Deployment
   109→
   110→The project ships as a single Docker image that bundles the React frontend and FastAPI backend. A multi-stage `Dockerfile` builds the frontend, then copies the output into the backend's static directory.
   111→
   112→### Build and run locally
   113→
   114→```bash
   115→docker build -t duckdb-data-agent .
   116→
   117→docker run -p 10000:10000 \
   118→  -e ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=sk-ant-... \
   119→  duckdb-data-agent
   120→```
   121→
   122→Add `-e LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY=pk-lf-... -e LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY=sk-lf-...` to either command to enable Langfuse tracing.
   123→
   124→Open http://localhost:10000 to use the app.
   125→
   126→### Deploy to Render
   127→
   128→A `render.yaml` is included for one-click deployment on [Render](https://render.com/):
   129→
   130→1. Push this repo to GitHub.
   131→2. In Render, create a new **Blueprint** and connect the repo.
   132→3. Set `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in the Render dashboard. Optionally set `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` to override the default model (`sonnet`). To enable Langfuse tracing, also set `LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY` and `LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY`.
   133→
   134→Render will build the Docker image and deploy it automatically on every push to `main`.
   135→
   136→## Security
   137→
   138→### Credential Proxy
   139→
   140→When the agent runs, the backend spawns a Claude Code subprocess via the Anthropic Agent SDK. A naive approach would pass `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` directly into that subprocess's environment — but any tool or shell command the agent executes could then read and exfiltrate the key.
   141→
   142→Instead, the backend runs a built-in reverse proxy at `/anthropic` that sits between Claude Code and `api.anthropic.com`:
   143→
   144→```
   145→Claude Code subprocess
   146→  → ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:{PORT}/anthropic
   147→  → ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=<short-lived UUID token>
   148→        ↓
   149→FastAPI proxy (/anthropic/{path})
   150→  → validates UUID token
   151→  → swaps it for the real ANTHROPIC_API_KEY
   152→  → forwards request to api.anthropic.com
   153→```
   154→
   155→**How it works:**
   156→
   157→1. Before each agent session, the backend mints a random UUID token with a 10-minute TTL.
   158→2. The token is injected into the subprocess environment as `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`; the real key is never exposed.
   159→3. The proxy validates every inbound request against the token store and substitutes the real key before forwarding upstream.
   160→4. When the session ends, the token is explicitly revoked in a `finally` block, regardless of success or error.
   161→5. A background task runs every 60 seconds to sweep any tokens that outlived their TTL.
   162→
   163→The subprocess only ever holds a single-session UUID. Even if a tool call reads the environment, all it gets is a temporary token scoped to that conversation.
   164→
   165→## Project Structure
   166→
   167→```
   168→├── frontend/               # React frontend
   169→│   ├── src/
   170→│   │   ├── components/     #   UI components (editor, results, sidebar, chat)
   171→│   │   ├── contexts/       #   React context providers (theme, language, agent, config, session)
   172→│   │   ├── hooks/          #   Custom hooks (useTheme, useTranslation, useAgent, useConfig)
   173→│   │   ├── agent/          #   Agent service (SSE event handling, session ID injection)
   174→│   │   ├── i18n/           #   Translation files (en.json, zh-TW.json)
   175→│   │   └── types.ts        #   Shared TypeScript interfaces
   176→│   ├── index.html          #   HTML entry point
   177→│   ├── package.json        #   npm config
   178→│   └── vite.config.ts      #   Vite bundler config
   179→├── backend/                # FastAPI backend
   180→│   └── app/
   181→│       ├── main.py         #   App setup, CORS, and background session cleanup loop
   182→│       ├── config.py       #   Environment variables (API key, model, upload limits)
   183→│       ├── database.py     #   DuckDB connection, query execution, and per-user SessionManager
   184→│       ├── agent.py        #   Agent loop & SSE streaming
   185→│       ├── proxy.py        #   Credential proxy: token store + /anthropic reverse proxy
   186→│       ├── tracing.py      #   Langfuse client wrapper & initialization
   187→│       ├── tools.py        #   Agent SDK tool definitions (execute_sql)
   188→│       ├── data/           #   Sample datasets (titanic.csv)
   189→│       └── routes/         #   API endpoints (tables, query, chat, config, langfuse status, heartbeat)
   190→├── Dockerfile              # Multi-stage production build
   191→├── render.yaml             # Render deployment config
   192→└── Makefile                # Dev commands (install, dev, clean)
   193→```
   194→
   195→## Tech Stack
   196→
   197→**Frontend**
   198→- [React](https://react.dev/) 18 + [TypeScript](https://www.typescriptlang.org/)
   199→- [Vite](https://vite.dev/)
   200→
   201→**Backend**
   202→- [FastAPI](https://fastapi.tiangolo.com/) + [Uvicorn](https://www.uvicorn.org/)
   203→- [DuckDB](https://duckdb.org/) (Python)
   204→- [Anthropic Agent SDK](https://github.com/anthropics/anthropic-sdk-python)
   205→- [Langfuse](https://langfuse.com/) (optional, for observability)
   206→
   207→## License
   208→
   209→[MIT](LICENSE.txt)
   210→
